The cross_attack_baseline registry entry is actually the old v4c-based evaluation that's been invalidated, while plan_006 represents the new plan_002-based evaluation that should replace it.

5. **All GPUs idle**: 4× RTX PRO 6000 sitting idle. With DDL 2026-05-25 (8 days), critical P0 experiments are still incomplete.

6. **plan_006 DD baseline anomaly**: Baseline recall on DD drops monotonically from K=1 to full (0.39→0.20→0.11→0.06→0.04), which is highly unusual—the baseline becomes increasingly confident that DD conversations are benign as we expand the context window, which doesn't align with expected behavior.

This could stem from a few possibilities: vanilla DeBERTa might be picking up on Crescendo-specific surface patterns that DD's different structure confuses it on with more context, there could be a GRU padding or masking issue at different sequence lengths, or the 38 benign samples in the dataset might be dominating the signal differently across K values.

Now I need to prepare my reports with the critical findings: WildChat's TF-IDF=0.998 confirms inherent lexical separability with real data, plan_004 is incomplete with 74/76 perturbed samples and no evaluation, all 4 GPUs are idle with only 8 days until the deadline, the experiment_plan.yaml has zero registry_id mappings breaking our tracking, and the plan_006 baseline DD anomaly needs investigation.

{
  "text": "[severity: warning] plan_006 跨攻击泛化结果分析 — DD baseline 异常行为需调查\n\n## 结果摘要（plan_006_cross_attack_eval.json）\n\n**FITD（Foot-in-the-Door）**：所有方法 F1≈1.0，无差异化。Treatment/Baseline/TF-IDF 都完美检测 FITD。\n\n**DD（Deceptive Delight）— 关键发现**：\n| K | Treatment F1 | Baseline F1 | TF-IDF F1 |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| 1 | 1.000 | 0.575 | 1.000 |\n| 2 | 0.990 | 0.513 | 0.981 |\n| 3 | 0.992 | 0.398 | 0.981 |\n| 5 | 1.000 | 0.310 | 0.990 |\n| full | 1.000 | 0.284 | 0.944 |\n\n## 异常：Baseline recall 随 K 增加而单调递减\nDD 上 baseline recall：K=1→0.39, K=2→0.20, K=3→0.11, K=5→0.06, full→0.04。**更多上下文使 baseline 更差**。这在正常分类器中是反常的。\n\n可能原因：\n1. **合理解释**：Baseline GRU 学到了 Crescendo 特有的表层模式（逐步升级）。DD 的结构（交错良性/恶意内容）与此模式不匹配，更多不匹配的 turn 使 GRU 更确信是良性。\n2. **可疑解释**：GRU 在不同序列长度下的 padding/masking 有 bug；或 38 个良性样本（来自 plan_002 test split）在不同 K 下的行为不一致。\n\n## 要点\n- Treatment vs baseline 在 DD 上的差距（+42pp 到 +72pp）是目前**最强的证据**，支持 persuasion grounding 的 OOD 价值\n- 但 TF-IDF 在 DD 上也达到 F1=0.94-1.0，削弱了\"需要 DisPeD\"的论证\n- 此实验**未注册在 registry 中**\n\n## 建议\n1. 注册 plan_006 到 registry\n2. 在 paper 引用 DD baseline 异常前，先验证 GRU 在不同 K 下的 padding 行为是否正确\n3. 论文叙事可定位为\"Treatment 在 OOD 场景下稳定（F1≈1.0），而 baseline 灾难性失败\"——但必须诚实承认 TF-IDF 也表现不错",
  "project": "persuasion_turn_jailbreak_detect"
}
```

{"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"path\": \"/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0514_2/projects/persuasion_turn_jailbreak_detect/paper_framing.md\", \"content\": \"# Paper Framing — persuasion_turn_jailbreak_detect\\n\\n## Target Venue\\n- Primary: EMNLP 2026 Main (ARR submission)\\n- Deadline: 2026-05-25 (ARR May cycle)\\n- Track: LLM Security / Discourse and Pragmatics\\n- Commitment deadline: 2026-08-02\\n\\n## Title (Working)\\nDisPeD: Exploiting the Pragmatic Evasion Asymmetry for Robust Multi-Turn Jailbreak Detection\\n\\n## Core Insight\\nPrior work established that persuasion is systematic in jailbreaks (Zeng et al. 2024), universal in successful attacks (Hagendorff 2026), and extractable from representations (arXiv 2508.05625). DisPeD operationalizes these observations into a working detection system by fine-tuning DeBERTa on persuasion strategy classification, producing turn embeddings that encode pragmatic function rather than (only) semantic content. The key insight is the Pragmatic Evasion Asymmetry: attackers can vary lexical/semantic content freely, but the discourse-level persuasion playbook (rapport → reframing → escalation) is a structural necessity that cannot be evaded without abandoning the attack.\\n\\n## Novelty Positioning (Dream-informed)\\nNOT: \\\"We discover that persuasion patterns exist in jailbreaks\\\" (already known).\\nYES: \\\"We operationalize persuasion structure into a detection signal, empirically validate an evasion hierarchy (lexical < semantic < pragmatic), and demonstrate that persuasion-grounded representations enable earlier detection and cross-attack generalization.\\\"\\n\\n## Inherent Lexical Separability (D009)\\nPersuasive jailbreak conversations and general benign conversations are inherently lexically separable (TF-IDF≈1.0 on ALL data sources: synthetic v4c, topic-anchored V2/V3, register-normalized, real WildChat). This is NOT a data quality issue — persuasive register (oral, hypothetical: \\\"what if\\\", \\\"just\\\", \\\"like\\\") differs fundamentally from informational register (formal, declarative: \\\"the\\\", \\\"is\\\", \\\"as\\\"). The paper ACKNOWLEDGES this and argues: lexical features work IID but fail OOD (cross-attack) and under adversarial perturbation. DisPeD's value is robustness and generalization, not IID accuracy.\\n\\n## Core Claims (3, priority-ordered)\\n\\n### Claim 1 (PRIMARY): Cross-Attack Generalization via Persuasion Grounding\\nPersuasion-grounded turn embeddings enable cross-attack-type generalization that vanilla semantic embeddings cannot achieve. A model trained on Crescendo detects Deceptive Delight attacks with F1≈1.0 while the vanilla baseline collapses to F1=0.28-0.58 (+42-72pp delta). This is because persuasion strategy structure (rapport → escalation) is shared across attack types, while surface lexical patterns are attack-specific.\\n- Evidence: plan_006 DD cross-attack eval — Treatment F1=0.99-1.0 vs Baseline 0.28-0.58\\n- Evidence (pending): scrambled cross-attack control — if scrambled model also fails on DD → persuasion labels specifically (not just fine-tuning) drive generalization\\n- Strength: strong empirical support\\n\\n### Claim 2 (ANALYSIS): Characteristic Persuasion Trajectories\\nDifferent multi-turn attack types exhibit distinct but recognizable persuasion strategy trajectories: Crescendo shows gradual escalation (mean strategy index 0.86→5.64), FITD follows commitment-escalation patterns, Deceptive Delight uses emotional-manipulation→role-assignment sequences. Benign conversations lack these structured escalation patterns. This provides both the mechanistic basis for early detection and native interpretability.\\n- Evidence: plan_008 strategy transition matrices (needs re-computation with topic-anchored benign as control)\\n- Strength: descriptive/analytical, supports Claim 1 mechanistically\\n\\n### Claim 3 (THEORETICAL FRAMING): Pragmatic Evasion Asymmetry\\nAn evasion hierarchy exists: lexical features (easily evaded) < semantic embeddings (moderate) < pragmatic persuasion patterns (structurally difficult to evade, as the persuasion playbook is a functional prerequisite for attack success). This frames WHY persuasion grounding works.\\n- Evidence IF plan_004 completes: adversarial perturbation showing treatment degrades less than baselines → upgrades to empirical\\n- Evidence WITHOUT plan_004: theoretical argument + TF-IDF feature analysis (A-type escalation markers as indirect evidence) → stays theoretical + preliminary\\n- Strength: theoretical contribution; empirical status depends on plan_004 completion within DDL\\n\\n## Story Spine\\n\\n**Problem**: Multi-turn jailbreak attacks (Crescendo, FITD, Deceptive Delight) distribute adversarial intent across individually benign turns. Bullwinkel et al. (2025) show per-turn representations are deliberately kept in a \\\"benign\\\" region, causing representation-level defenses to fail with a 50-point ASR gap. DeepContext (Su et al., arXiv 2602.16935, 2026) tracks generic semantic drift via RNN over BERT embeddings (F1=0.84) but confuses benign conversational evolution with adversarial manipulation.\\n\\n**Gap**: Existing detectors operate at the lexical or semantic level where attacks deliberately hide. Production multi-turn guards (Llama Guard, WildGuard, ShieldGemma) and LLM-as-judge approaches detect individual harmful outputs but not the adversarial manipulation process itself. No published work operationalizes persuasion-grounded turn representations with temporal sequence modeling for multi-turn jailbreak detection.\\n\\n**Our angle**: We operationalize the Pragmatic Evasion Asymmetry into a detection system. The persuasion playbook (rapport → reframing → escalation) is a structural necessity for multi-turn attack success. DisPeD fine-tunes DeBERTa on an 8-class persuasion taxonomy to produce pragmatic-aware turn embeddings, then models the temporal strategy sequence with a GRU to detect the characteristic escalation trajectory. Unlike prior work that observes persuasion in attacks, we demonstrate its utility as a robust detection signal with empirical validation of an evasion hierarchy.\\n\\n**Headline result**: \\\"DisPeD detects Crescendo-style jailbreaks at K=2 turns with F1=X vs Y for vanilla semantic baselines (+Z pp). Under adversarial paraphrasing that removes lexical cues, DisPeD maintains F1=W while lexical baselines degrade by V pp.\\\"\\n\\n## Architecture (Simplified for MVE)\\n\\nSingle-track with persuasion grounding (full dual-track in future work):\\n1. **Stage 1**: DeBERTa-v3-base fine-tuned on multi-task objective: 8-class persuasion strategy classification + binary jailbreak-intent classification\\n2. **Stage 2**: Bi-GRU over DeBERTa [CLS] embeddings for conversation-level binary classification\\n3. **Baseline**: Same pipeline but with vanilla (not fine-tuned) DeBERTa embeddings\\n\\n## Venue Fit Risks\\n- EMNLP 2026 LLM Security track: strong fit (dedicated track, high visibility)\\n- Discourse/Pragmatics reviewers may want more linguistic rigor: ground taxonomy in PAP (ACL 2024), Hagendorff (Nature Comm 2026), Cialdini principles, Speech Act Theory (Austin 1962, Searle 1969)\\n- Safety reviewers expect multiple attack types: need >= 2 attack families\\n\\n## Anticipated Reviewer Concerns\\n\\n### RC1: Lexical Shortcut (CRITICAL, must address pre-submission)\\nTF-IDF=1.0 on current data means lexical features alone perfectly separate classes. Register normalization failed after 6 iterations (D004, D006). Residual TF-IDF signal reflects genuine pragmatic-lexical co-occurrence, not trivial confound.\\n- Mitigation strategy (post-D006): (a) TF-IDF feature analysis showing residual signal is A-type (persuasion-inherent: \\\"what if\\\", \\\"you know\\\", rapport markers) not B-type confound; (b) plan_003 scrambled-label ablation proves model uses strategy ordering, not just lexical cues; (c) plan_004 adversarial perturbation proves lexical baseline collapses under paraphrasing while treatment is robust; (d) plan_006 cross-attack generalization — if model generalizes to unseen attack types, lexical memorization explanation fails\\n\\n### RC2: Small Dataset\\n480 samples is small for a detection paper.\\n- Mitigation: (a) statistical significance via bootstrap CI or paired tests; (b) cross-validation; (c) focus narrative on proof-of-concept for the principle, not SOTA numbers\\n\\n### RC3: Single Attack Type (Crescendo Only)\\nNeed generalization evidence.\\n- Mitigation: generate FITD-style and Deceptive Delight-style conversations using existing pipeline\\n\\n### RC4: Strategy Label Confound (M004)\\nBenign turns hardcoded to strategy=7 creates shortcut for treatment model.\\n- Mitigation: (a) fix labeling (use \\\"none\\\"/9th class); (b) scrambled-label ablation\\n\\n### RC5: No Production/LLM-as-Judge Baseline (Dream-surfaced)\\nWithout comparing against zero-shot LLM-based detection (e.g., GPT-4/Claude prompted for conversation-level jailbreak detection), practical positioning is unknown.\\n- Mitigation: add LLM-as-judge baseline experiment (plan_012)\\n\\n### RC6: Interpretability Claims Without Human Eval\\nStrategy sequence outputs claimed interpretable but not evaluated.\\n- Mitigation: strategy transition matrix visualization + predicted-label trajectory analysis\\n\\n### RC7: Related Work Inadequate (Dream-surfaced, THREE-TIER CONSISTENT)\\n~12 citations is far below venue norms (30-50). Missing coverage of: production safety systems (Llama Guard, WildGuard, ShieldGemma), LLM-as-judge, discourse analysis (RST, PDTB, dialogue acts), computational persuasion (Habernal & Gurevych 2016, Tan et al. 2016), red-teaming (HarmBench, Perez et al. 2022), annotation artifacts (Gururangan et al. 2018), adaptive attacks.\\n- Mitigation: expand to 30+ citations covering all missing categories (Paper Agent task)\\n\\n### RC8: Incremental Novelty Over Prior Work (Dream-surfaced, TWO-TIER)\\nPragmatic Evasion Asymmetry is a natural corollary of Zeng+Hagendorff+HowDoLLMs. Paper should position as \\\"operationalizing known observations\\\" not \\\"identifying a new phenomenon.\\\"\\n- Mitigation: reframe contribution as operationalization + empirical validation (done in novelty positioning above)\\n\\n### RC9: Multi-seed and Statistical Rigor (Dream-surfaced, THREE-TIER)\\nn=72 test set with single seed is inadequate. No cross-validation or multi-seed variance reported.\\n- Mitigation: (a) 3-5 random seeds for plan_002; (b) k-fold CV or proper bootstrap CIs\\n\\n### RC10: \\\"None\\\"-Collapse Ablation (Dream-surfaced, TWO-TIER)\\nIf benign turns are mostly \\\"none\\\" and jailbreak mostly non-\\\"none\\\", model may reduce to persuasion-presence detector rather than trajectory detector.\\n- Mitigation: merge 8 classes into binary \\\"persuasion-present/absent\\\" and retrain; if still works, acknowledge and adjust narrative\\n\\n### RC11: Lambda and Architecture Ablation (Dream-surfaced, TWO-TIER)\\nLambda=1.0 not ablated; Bi-GRU vs alternatives not compared.\\n- Mitigation: (a) lambda in {0, 0.5, 1.0, 2.0}; (b) at minimum: mean pooling comparison\\n\\n## Scope Boundary\\n- IN: persuasion-mediated multi-turn attacks (Crescendo, FITD, Deceptive Delight, social engineering jailbreaks)\\n- OUT (acknowledged limitation): pure information accumulation attacks, token smuggling, single-frame roleplay\\n- The semantic GRU track provides coverage for out-of-scope attack types as a safety net\\n\\n## Theoretical Grounding\\n### Core References\\n- Bullwinkel et al. (arXiv 2507.02956): per-turn representations kept \\\"benign\\\" by multi-turn attacks\\n- Speech Act Theory (Austin 1962, Searle 1969): existing detectors at locutionary act level, DisPeD at illocutionary act level\\n- PAP (Zeng et al., ACL 2024): 40 persuasion techniques for jailbreaking\\n- Hagendorff (Nature Communications 2026): identifiable persuasion strategies in 97% ASR attacks\\n- \\\"How Do LLMs Persuade?\\\" (arXiv 2508.05625): persuasion dynamics extractable from turn-level representations\\n\\n### Must-Cite Categories (Dream-identified gaps)\\n- **Production safety**: Llama Guard 2/3, ShieldGemma, WildGuard, Constitutional Classifiers\\n- **Multi-turn defenses**: BIID, G-Guard, SafeDream\\n- **Discourse analysis**: RST (Mann & Thompson 1988), PDTB, dialogue act classification (Stolcke et al. 2000)\\n- **Computational persuasion**: Habernal & Gurevych 2016, Tan et al. 2016\\n- **Red-teaming**: Perez et al. 2022, Ganguli et al. 2022, HarmBench (Mazeika et al. 2024)\\n- **Adaptive attacks**: Nasr & Carlini et al. 2025\\n- **Annotation artifacts**: Gururangan et al. 2018, Niven & Kao 2019, Poliak et al. 2018\\n- **Jailbreak taxonomies**: Wei et al. 2024, Chao et al. 2023 (PAIR)\\n\", \"total_chars\": 12209, \"truncated\": false}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {}}"}

No cross-validation or multi-seed variance reported.\\\\n- Mitigation: (a) 3-5 random seeds for plan_002; (b) k-fold CV or proper bootstrap CIs\\\\n\\\\n### RC10: \\\\\\\"None\\\\\\\"-Collapse Ablation (Dream-surfaced, TWO-TIER)\\\\nIf benign turns are mostly \\\\\\\"none\\\\\\\" and jailbreak mostly non-\\\\\\\"none\\\\\\\", model may reduce to persuasion-presence detector rather than trajectory detector.\\\\n- Mitigation: merge 8 classes into binary \\\\\\\"persuasion-present/absent\\\\\\\" and retrain; if still works, acknowledge and adjust narrative\\\\n\\\\n### RC11: Lambda and Architecture Ablation (Dream-surfaced, TWO-TIER)\\\\nLambda=1.0 not ablated; Bi-GRU vs alternatives not compared.\\\\n- Mitigation: (a) lambda in {0, 0.5, 1.0, 2.0}; (b) at minimum: mean pooling comparison\\\\n\\\\n## Scope Boundary\\\\n- IN: persuasion-mediated multi-turn attacks (Crescendo, FITD, Deceptive Delight, social engineering jailbreaks)\\\\n- OUT (acknowledged limitation): pure information accumulation attacks, token smuggling, single-frame roleplay\\\\n- The semantic GRU track provides coverage for out-of-scope attack types as a safety net\\\\n\\\\n## Theoretical Grounding\\\\n### Core References\\\\n- Bullwinkel et al.       "result": "{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"path\": \"/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0514_2/projects/persuasion_turn_jailbreak_detect/paper_framing.md\", \"content\": \"# Paper Framing — persuasion_turn_jailbreak_detect\\n\\n## Target Venue\\n- Primary: EMNLP 2026 Main (ARR submission)\\n- Deadline: 2026-05-25 (ARR May cycle)\\n- Track: LLM Security / Discourse and Pragmatics\\n- Commitment deadline: 2026-08-02\\n\\n## Title (Working)\\nDisPeD: Exploiting the Pragmatic Evasion Asymmetry for Robust Multi-Turn Jailbreak Detection\\n\\n## Core Insight\\nPrior work established that persuasion is systematic in jailbreaks (Zeng et al. 2024), universal in successful attacks (Hagendorff 2026), and extractable from representations (arXiv 2508.05625). DisPeD operationalizes these observations into a working detection system by fine-tuning DeBERTa on persuasion strategy classification, producing turn embeddings that encode pragmatic function rather than (only) semantic content. The key insight is the Pragmatic Evasion Asymmetry: attackers can vary lexical/semantic content freely, but the discourse-level persuasion playbook (rapport → reframing → escalation) is a structural necessity that cannot be evaded without abandoning the attack.\\n\\n## Novelty Positioning (Dream-informed)\\nNOT: \\\"We discover that persuasion patterns exist in jailbreaks\\\" (already known).\\nYES: \\\"We operationalize persuasion structure into a detection signal, empirically validate an evasion hierarchy (lexical < semantic < pragmatic), and demonstrate that persuasion-grounded representations enable earlier detection and cross-attack generalization.\\\"\\n\\n## Inherent Lexical Separability (D009)\\nPersuasive jailbreak conversations and general benign conversations are inherently lexically separable (TF-IDF≈1.0 on ALL data sources: synthetic v4c, topic-anchored V2/V3, register-normalized, real WildChat). This is NOT a data quality issue — persuasive register (oral, hypothetical: \\\"what if\\\", \\\"just\\\", \\\"like\\\") differs fundamentally from informational register (formal, declarative: \\\"the\\\", \\\"is\\\", \\\"as\\\"). The paper ACKNOWLEDGES this and argues: lexical features work IID but fail OOD (cross-attack) and under adversarial perturbation. DisPeD's value is robustness and generalization, not IID accuracy.\\n\\n## Core Claims (3, priority-ordered)\\n\\n### Claim 1 (PRIMARY): Cross-Attack Generalization via Persuasion Grounding\\nPersuasion-grounded turn embeddings enable cross-attack-type generalization that vanilla semantic embeddings cannot achieve. A model trained on Crescendo detects Deceptive Delight attacks with F1≈1.0 while the vanilla baseline collapses to F1=0.28-0.58 (+42-72pp delta). This is because persuasion strategy structure (rapport → escalation) is shared across attack types, while surface lexical patterns are attack-specific.\\n- Evidence: plan_006 DD cross-attack eval — Treatment F1=0.99-1.0 vs Baseline 0.28-0.58\\n- Evidence (pending): scrambled cross-attack control — if scrambled model also fails on DD → persuasion labels specifically (not just fine-tuning) drive generalization\\n- Strength: strong empirical support\\n\\n### Claim 2 (ANALYSIS): Characteristic Persuasion Trajectories\\nDifferent multi-turn attack types exhibit distinct but recognizable persuasion strategy trajectories: Crescendo shows gradual escalation (mean strategy index 0.86→5.64), FITD follows commitment-escalation patterns, Deceptive Delight uses emotional-manipulation→role-assignment sequences. Benign conversations lack these structured escalation patterns. This provides both the mechanistic basis for early detection and native interpretability.\\n- Evidence: plan_008 strategy transition matrices (needs re-computation with topic-anchored benign as control)\\n- Strength: descriptive/analytical, supports Claim 1 mechanistically\\n\\n### Claim 3 (THEORETICAL FRAMING): Pragmatic Evasion Asymmetry\\nAn evasion hierarchy exists: lexical features (easily evaded) < semantic embeddings (moderate) < pragmatic persuasion patterns (structurally difficult to evade, as the persuasion playbook is a functional prerequisite for attack success). This frames WHY persuasion grounding works.\\n- Evidence IF plan_004 completes: adversarial perturbation showing treatment degrades less than baselines → upgrades to empirical\\n- Evidence WITHOUT plan_004: theoretical argument + TF-IDF feature analysis (A-type escalation markers as indirect evidence) → stays theoretical + preliminary\\n- Strength: theoretical contribution; empirical status depends on plan_004 completion within DDL\\n\\n## Story Spine\\n\\n**Problem**: Multi-turn jailbreak attacks (Crescendo, FITD, Deceptive Delight) distribute adversarial intent across individually benign turns. Bullwinkel et al. (2025) show per-turn representations are deliberately kept in a \\\"benign\\\" region, causing representation-level defenses to fail with a 50-point ASR gap. DeepContext (Su et al., arXiv 2602.16935, 2026) tracks generic semantic drift via RNN over BERT embeddings (F1=0.84) but confuses benign conversational evolution with adversarial manipulation.\\n\\n**Gap**: Existing detectors operate at the lexical or semantic level where attacks deliberately hide. Production multi-turn guards (Llama Guard, WildGuard, ShieldGemma) and LLM-as-judge approaches detect individual harmful outputs but not the adversarial manipulation process itself. No published work operationalizes persuasion-grounded turn representations with temporal sequence modeling for multi-turn jailbreak detection.\\n\\n**Our angle**: We operationalize the Pragmatic Evasion Asymmetry into a detection system. The persuasion playbook (rapport → reframing → escalation) is a structural necessity for multi-turn attack success. DisPeD fine-tunes DeBERTa on an 8-class persuasion taxonomy to produce pragmatic-aware turn embeddings, then models the temporal strategy sequence with a GRU to detect the characteristic escalation trajectory. Unlike prior work that observes persuasion in attacks, we demonstrate its utility as a robust detection signal with empirical validation of an evasion hierarchy.\\n\\n**Headline result**: \\\"DisPeD detects Crescendo-style jailbreaks at K=2 turns with F1=X vs Y for vanilla semantic baselines (+Z pp). Under adversarial paraphrasing that removes lexical cues, DisPeD maintains F1=W while lexical baselines degrade by V pp.\\\"\\n\\n## Architecture (Simplified for MVE)\\n\\nSingle-track with persuasion grounding (full dual-track in future work):\\n1. **Stage 1**: DeBERTa-v3-base fine-tuned on multi-task objective: 8-class persuasion strategy classification + binary jailbreak-intent classification\\n2. **Stage 2**: Bi-GRU over DeBERTa [CLS] embeddings for conversation-level binary classification\\n3. **Baseline**: Same pipeline but with vanilla (not fine-tuned) DeBERTa embeddings\\n\\n## Venue Fit Risks\\n- EMNLP 2026 LLM Security track: strong fit (dedicated track, high visibility)\\n- Discourse/Pragmatics reviewers may want more linguistic rigor: ground taxonomy in PAP (ACL 2024), Hagendorff (Nature Comm 2026), Cialdini principles, Speech Act Theory (Austin 1962, Searle 1969)\\n- Safety reviewers expect multiple attack types: need >= 2 attack families\\n\\n## Anticipated Reviewer Concerns\\n\\n### RC1: Lexical Shortcut (CRITICAL, must address pre-submission)\\nTF-IDF=1.0 on current data means lexical features alone perfectly separate classes. Register normalization failed after 6 iterations (D004, D006). Residual TF-IDF signal reflects genuine pragmatic-lexical co-occurrence, not trivial confound.\\n- Mitigation strategy (post-D006): (a) TF-IDF feature analysis showing residual signal is A-type (persuasion-inherent: \\\"what if\\\", \\\"you know\\\", rapport markers) not B-type confound; (b) plan_003 scrambled-label ablation proves model uses strategy ordering, not just lexical cues; (c) plan_004 adversarial perturbation proves lexical baseline collapses under paraphrasing while treatment is robust; (d) plan_006 cross-attack generalization — if model generalizes to unseen attack types, lexical memorization explanation fails\\n\\n### RC2: Small Dataset\\n480 samples is small for a detection paper.\\n- Mitigation: (a) statistical significance via bootstrap CI or paired tests; (b) cross-validation; (c) focus narrative on proof-of-concept for the principle, not SOTA numbers\\n\\n### RC3: Single Attack Type (Crescendo Only)\\nNeed generalization evidence.\\n- Mitigation: generate FITD-style and Deceptive Delight-style conversations using existing pipeline\\n\\n### RC4: Strategy Label Confound (M004)\\nBenign turns hardcoded to strategy=7 creates shortcut for treatment model.\\n- Mitigation: (a) fix labeling (use \\\"none\\\"/9th class); (b) scrambled-label ablation\\n\\n### RC5: No Production/LLM-as-Judge Baseline (Dream-surfaced)\\nWithout comparing against zero-shot LLM-based detection (e.g., GPT-4/Claude prompted for conversation-level jailbreak detection), practical positioning is unknown.\\n- Mitigation: add LLM-as-judge baseline experiment (plan_012)\\n\\n### RC6: Interpretability Claims Without Human Eval\\nStrategy sequence outputs claimed interpretable but not evaluated.\\n- Mitigation: strategy transition matrix visualization + predicted-label trajectory analysis\\n\\n### RC7: Related Work Inadequate (Dream-surfaced, THREE-TIER CONSISTENT)\\n~12 citations is far below venue norms (30-50). Missing coverage of: production safety systems (Llama Guard, WildGuard, ShieldGemma), LLM-as-judge, discourse analysis (RST, PDTB, dialogue acts), computational persuasion (Habernal & Gurevych 2016, Tan et al. 2016), red-teaming (HarmBench, Perez et al. 2022), annotation artifacts (Gururangan et al. 2018), adaptive attacks.\\n- Mitigation: expand to 30+ citations covering all missing categories (Paper Agent task)\\n\\n### RC8: Incremental Novelty Over Prior Work (Dream-surfaced, TWO-TIER)\\nPragmatic Evasion Asymmetry is a natural corollary of Zeng+Hagendorff+HowDoLLMs. Paper should position as \\\"operationalizing known observations\\\" not \\\"identifying a new phenomenon.\\\"\\n- Mitigation: reframe contribution as operationalization + empirical validation (done in novelty positioning above)\\n\\n### RC9: Multi-seed and Statistical Rigor (Dream-surfaced, THREE-TIER)\\nn=72 test set with single seed is inadequate. No cross-validation or multi-seed variance reported.\\n- Mitigation: (a) 3-5 random seeds for plan_002; (b) k-fold CV or proper bootstrap CIs\\n\\n### RC10: \\\"None\\\"-Collapse Ablation (Dream-surfaced, TWO-TIER)\\nIf benign turns are mostly \\\"none\\\" and jailbreak mostly non-\\\"none\\\", model may reduce to persuasion-presence detector rather than trajectory detector.\\n- Mitigation: merge 8 classes into binary \\\"persuasion-present/absent\\\" and retrain; if still works, acknowledge and adjust narrative\\n\\n### RC11: Lambda and Architecture Ablation (Dream-surfaced, TWO-TIER)\\nLambda=1.0 not ablated; Bi-GRU vs alternatives not compared.\\n- Mitigation: (a) lambda in {0, 0.5, 1.0, 2.0}; (b) at minimum: mean pooling comparison\\n\\n## Scope Boundary\\n- IN: persuasion-mediated multi-turn attacks (Crescendo, FITD, Deceptive Delight, social engineering jailbreaks)\\n- OUT (acknowledged limitation): pure information accumulation attacks, token smuggling, single-frame roleplay\\n- The semantic GRU track provides coverage for out-of-scope attack types as a safety net\\n\\n## Theoretical Grounding\\n### Core References\\n- Bullwinkel et al. (arXiv 2507.02956): per-turn representations kept \\\"benign\\\" by multi-turn attacks\\n- Speech Act Theory (Austin 1962, Searle 1969): existing detectors at locutionary act level, DisPeD at illocutionary act level\\n- PAP (Zeng et al., ACL 2024): 40 persuasion techniques for jailbreaking\\n- Hagendorff (Nature Communications 2026): identifiable persuasion strategies in 97% ASR attacks\\n- \\\"How Do LLMs Persuade?\\\" (arXiv 2508.05625): persuasion dynamics extractable from turn-level representations\\n\\n### Must-Cite Categories (Dream-identified gaps)\\n- **Production safety**: Llama Guard 2/3, ShieldGemma, WildGuard, Constitutional Classifiers\\n- **Multi-turn defenses**: BIID, G-Guard, SafeDream\\n- **Discourse analysis**: RST (Mann & Thompson 1988), PDTB, dialogue act classification (Stolcke et al. 2000)\\n- **Computational persuasion**: Habernal & Gurevych 2016, Tan et al. 2016\\n- **Red-teaming**: Perez et al. 2022, Ganguli et al. 2022, HarmBench (Mazeika et al. 2024)\\n- **Adaptive attacks**: Nasr & Carlini et al. 2025\\n- **Annotation artifacts**: Gururangan et al. 2018, Niven & Kao 2019, Poliak et al. 2018\\n- **Jailbreak taxonomies**: Wei et al. 2024, Chao et al. 2023 (PAIR)\\n\", \"total_chars\": 12209, \"truncated\": false}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {}}"

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Good. The checkpoints are symlinks pointing to plan_002 checkpoints. They all exist and are valid:
- plan_004_treatment/deberta/model.pt → plan_002/deberta_multitask/best/model.pt
- plan_004_treatment/gru/best.pt → plan_002/gru/treatment/best.pt
- plan_004_baseline/gru/best.pt → plan_002/gru/baseline/best.pt

So the plan_004 evaluation can be run. The perturbation data is ready (76/76), checkpoints are linked. Just needs the eval command.


Now I'm doing a coverage audit for the paper. The cross-attack generalization claim shows strong results in plan_006 with treatment F1=1.0 versus baseline F1=0.28-0.58 on DD, but TF-IDF achieves F1=0.94-1.0 on the same data, which weakens the argument for needing DisPeD. There's also a concern that plan_006 uses a model trained on confounded data where TF-IDF already achieves perfect performance, so I need to verify whether the treatment model is actually learning something meaningful beyond what the baseline features capture.

For the persuasion trajectories claim, plan_008 shows the crescendo escalation pattern clearly with mean indices jumping from 0.86 to 5.64, and the FITD/DD patterns are distinct, but I should recompute this with a proper benign control using topic-anchored benign data. The pragmatic evasion asymmetry claim is still theoretical since plan_004 has the perturbation data ready but the evaluation hasn't run yet—that experiment would be crucial for moving this from theory to empirical evidence. Plan_002 gave a negative result with maximum delta of 1.3 percentage points, falling short of the 5 percentage point threshold for IID robustness.

Looking at the additional results, plan_003 showed scrambled and proper perform identically on the confounded data as expected, plan_012 revealed Qwen3-8B's weak performance but that was on the wrong test split, and WildChat's TF-IDF score of 0.998 confirms the inherent separability of the data. I'm tracking several critical gaps: plan_013 on the None-collapse ablation hasn't started, the 3-seed validation is missing, plan_004 still needs evaluation, and plan_012 needs to be rerun on the correct data splits. Beyond those, I also need to start plan_014 for lambda ablation, plan_015 for the mean pooling baseline, and plan_009 for WildChat hard negatives before the deadline.

Now I'm auditing what's actually needed for a viable paper given the 2026-05-25 deadline. The core problem is that I've committed to 3-seed validation but haven't executed it—running on a single seed with only 72 test samples is a significant limitation. Plan_004 evaluation is still theoretical without actual results, the None-collapse mechanism in plan_013 remains unanswered, plan_012's baseline was evaluated on the wrong data split, and plan_006's core evidence wasn't properly tracked. These gaps directly undermine the paper's claims and need to be addressed before submission.

Looking at the GPU budget, I have 768 GPU-hours available across the remaining 8 days, and the critical experiments need roughly 41 hours—plan_004 evaluation, plan_013, rerunning plan_012, and completing the 3-seed validation for plan_002. That's well within budget, so the real bottleneck is implementation time for the Agent to execute these experiments, not computational resources.

Now I'm moving on to drafting the comprehensive audit report and writing up the paper.
```

  "text": "[severity: warning] DDL-8 综合覆盖度与论文就绪度审查\n\n## 一、Claim 证据覆盖\n\n### Claim 1 (PRIMARY): Cross-Attack Generalization\n- ✅ plan_006 DD 上 Treatment F1=1.0 vs Baseline F1=0.28-0.58（+42-72pp delta）— 强证据\n- ⚠️ TF-IDF 在 DD 上也达 F1=0.94-1.0，削弱\"需要 DisPeD\"论证\n- ⚠️ Baseline DD recall 随 K 增加单调递减（0.39→0.04）— 异常行为，需排除 padding/masking bug\n- ⚠️ plan_006 未注册到 registry\n- ❌ 无 scrambled cross-attack control（如果 scrambled 模型在 DD 上也 F1=1.0 → persuasion labels 无功）\n\n### Claim 2 (ANALYSIS): Characteristic Persuasion Trajectories\n- ✅ plan_008 完成，Crescendo 单调升级模式已确认\n- ⚠️ 缺良性对照的策略转移矩阵\n\n### Claim 3 (THEORETICAL → EMPIRICAL?): Pragmatic Evasion Asymmetry\n- ✅ 扰动数据 76/76 就绪\n- ❌ plan_004 评估未运行 — Claim 3 停在 theoretical\n\n## 二、关键缺口（按优先级排）\n\n| 优先 | 缺口 | 影响 | GPU-hrs |\n|------|------|------|---------|\n| P0 | plan_004 eval 未跑 | Claim 3 无法升级 empirical | 0.5 |\n| P0 | 3-seed 验证（D007 承诺） | RC9 直接扣分 | 36 |\n| P0 | plan_013 None-collapse ablation | RC10 无答案 | 4 |\n| P0 | plan_012 数据不一致 | LLM baseline 不可引用 | 0.5 |\n| P1 | plan_014 Lambda ablation | RC11 部分 | 6 |\n| P1 | plan_015 Mean pooling baseline | RC11 部分 | 2 |\n\n## 三、GPU 预算\n- 剩余可用：~768 GPU-hours（4×8天×24h）\n- 剩余 P0 需求：~41 GPU-hours\n- **GPU 不是瓶颈**，瓶颈是实验编排和执行速度\n\n## 四、流程问题\n1. eval_early_detection.py 211-216 行硬编码 stage1 metrics 仍未修复\n2. experiment_plan.yaml 所有 registry_id 为 null\n3. report.md 显示\"无活跃 idea\"、\"无运行中实验\"、\"无 GPU 信息\" — 状态记录严重滞后\n\n## 五、最小可行论文评估\n如果只完成 plan_004 eval + plan_012 rerun，论文有：\n- Cross-attack generalization（Claim 1，强）\n- Adversarial robustness（Claim 3，升级 empirical）\n- Strategy trajectories（Claim 2，中等）\n- LLM-as-judge baseline（RC5）\n\n但仍缺：3-seed（RC9 致命）、None-collapse（RC10 重要）、Lambda/architecture ablation（RC11 中等）\n\n## 建议执行顺序\n1. **立即**：plan_004 eval（0.5h）+ plan_012 rerun on plan_002 data（0.5h）— 可并行\n2. **今天内**：plan_002 seed 2+3（可 4 GPU 并行缩短到 9h）\n3. **明天**：plan_013 None-collapse（4h）\n4. **余量**：plan_014 + plan_015",

embs, labels, lengths, device):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Run GRU classifier on precomputed embeddings.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    gru_model.eval()\\n    max_len = max(lengths)\\n    embed_dim = embs[0].size(1)\\n    \\n    padded = torch.zeros(len(embs), max_len, embed_dim)\\n    for i, e in enumerate(embs):\\n        padded[i, :e.size(0), :] = e\\n    \\n    lengths_tensor = torch.tensor(lengths, dtype=torch.long)\\n    labels_tensor = torch.tensor(labels, dtype=torch.long)\\n    \\n    with torch.no_grad():\\n        padded = padded.to(device)\\n        lengths_tensor_dev = lengths_tensor.to(device)\\n        logits = gru_model(padded, lengths_tensor_dev)\\n        preds = logits.argmax(-1).cpu().numpy()\\n        probs = torch.softmax(logits, dim=-1)[:, 1].cpu().numpy()\\n    \\n    labels_np = labels_tensor.numpy()\\n    metrics = {\\n        \\\"f1\\\": f1_score(labels_np, preds, zero_division=0),\\n        \\\"precision\\\": precision_score(labels_np, preds, zero_division=0),\\n        \\\"recall\\\": recall_score(labels_np, preds, zero_division=0),\\n        \\\"accuracy\\\": accuracy_score(labels_np, preds),\\n    }\\n    \\n    # FPR@95TPR\\n    tpr_threshold = 0.95\\n    benign_probs = probs[labels_np == 0]\\n    jailbreak_probs = probs[labels_np == 1]\\n    \\n    if len(jailbreak_probs) > 0 and len(benign_probs) > 0:\\n        thresholds = np.sort(jailbreak_probs)\\n        idx = max(0, int(np.floor(len(thresholds) * (1 - tpr_threshold))) - 1)\\n        threshold_at_95tpr = thresholds[idx]\\n        fpr = evaluate_tfidf(train_convs, test_convs, k):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"TF-IDF + Logistic Regression baseline.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    def make_texts(convs, k):\\n        texts, labels = [], []\\n        for conv in _source line 1361 · 2026-05-17T23:40:37.274Z_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Critical findings from the worker:

1. **Perturbation step completed successfully** (76/76 conversations, 632 turns perturbed)
2. **Quality metrics look good**:
   - BERTScore F1: 0.9237 (high semantic preservation)
   - Semantic Similarity: 0.7437 (decent meaning retention)
   - Lexical Overlap: 0.1962 (low — good, means aggressive paraphrasing worked)
   - 0 low-quality turns filtered
3. **plan_006 scrambled DD eval completed**: Scrambled model on DD gets F1=0.93-0.99 — VERY important finding


4. **plan_004 evaluation hit a checkpoint mismatch error** — the loaded model expects a 9-class persuasion head but the current architecture only has 8 classes, causing the weight and bias tensors to fail loading.

The root cause is that plan_002 was trained with 9 persuasion classes (8 strategies plus "none"), but `adversarial_perturbation.py` instantiates the model with the default 8-class configuration. This blocks the evaluation of the perturbation data entirely.

Now examining the scrambled DD results, where the performance metrics show strong F1 scores across different K values — K=1 achieves 0.990 with treatment at 1.0, K=2 drops slightly to 0.935, and K=3 maintains similar performance around 0.935.

This reveals something critical: the scrambled model (trained on randomly shuffled persuasion labels) performs nearly as well as the properly-labeled treatment on DD, with only a 1-6 percentage point gap. This suggests the cross-attack generalization benefit isn't actually coming from learning correct persuasion strategy labels — the fine-tuning works almost equally well with random labels.

The real mechanism appears to be exposure to persuasion-related text during fine-tuning itself, not the specific structure of the 8-class taxonomy. This fundamentally challenges Claim 1 as currently stated, since the persuasion strategy structure isn't what's driving the generalization advantage. I need to flag this immediately along with the plan_004 evaluation crash from the class mismatch.
```

{
  "text": "[severity: critical] Scrambled DD 结果严重削弱 Claim 1 的因果论证 — persuasion labels 非关键\n\n## 发现\nplan_006_scrambled_dd_eval.json 显示 scrambled 模型（随机打乱 persuasion 策略标签）在 DD 上的表现：\n\n| K | Treatment F1 | Scrambled F1 | Baseline F1 | TF-IDF F1 |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| 1 | 1.000 | 0.990 | 0.575 | 1.000 |\n| 2 | 0.990 | 0.935 | 0.513 | 0.981 |\n| 3 | 0.992 | 0.935 | 0.398 | 0.981 |\n| 5 | 1.000 | 0.990 | 0.310 | 0.990 |\n| full | 1.000 | 0.981 | 0.284 | 0.944 |\n\nTreatment vs Scrambled 差距仅 1-6pp。Scrambled 模型已经大幅超越 Baseline（+36-70pp on DD）。\n\n## 因果论证断裂\nClaim 1 当前表述：\"Persuasion-grounded turn embeddings enable cross-attack-type generalization\"。但数据显示：\n1. **正确的 persuasion labels 不是关键** — scrambled labels 几乎同样有效\n2. **关键是 DeBERTa fine-tuning 过程本身** — 在 persuasion 相关文本上微调（无论标签正确与否）就足以产生 OOD 泛化能力\n3. **Baseline 崩溃的原因** 不是缺少 persuasion 策略信息，而是缺少 fine-tuning（vanilla DeBERTa 的表征空间没有针对攻击文本优化）\n\n## 对论文的影响\n这不是灾难性的，但需要诚实重新定位：\n- ❌ 不能说\"persuasion strategy labels drive generalization\"\n- ✅ 可以说\"fine-tuning on persuasion-annotated data produces representations that capture attack-relevant pragmatic features beyond surface lexical patterns\"\n- ✅ 可以说\"the multi-task persuasion objective provides a natural supervision signal for attack-aware representations\"\n- Treatment vs Scrambled 的 1-6pp 差距在统计上可能不显著（n=80 DD + 38 benign, single seed）\n\n## 建议\n1. paper_framing.md 的 Claim 1 需要从\"strategy labels drive generalization\"调整为\"persuasion-related fine-tuning enables generalization\"\n2. 8-class taxonomy 的价值可以保留在 Claim 2（interpretability/trajectory analysis），而非 Claim 1（detection performance）\n3. 这反而可以加强论文的某个角度：即使不需要精确的策略标签，persuasion-aware 微调目标也足以产生鲁棒表征 — 降低了方法的实际部署门槛",
  "project": "persuasion_turn_jailbreak_detect"
}

enc.parameters():\\n        p.requires_grad = False\\n    return enc\\n\\n\\ndef load_gru(path, device, embed_dim=768):\\n    gru = GRUClassifier(input_dim=embed_dim, hidden_dim=256, num_layers=2, dropout=0.3)\\n    gru.load_state_dict(torch.load(path, map_location=\\\"cpu\\\"))\\n    gru.to(device).eval()\\n    return gru\\n\\n\\ndef embed_turns(encoder, tokenizer, turns, device, k=None):\\n    t = turns[:k] if k is not None else turns\\n    if len(t) == 0:\\n        t = [\\\"\\\"]\\n    enc = tokenizer(t, max_length=MAX_LENGTH, padding=True, truncation=True, out.last_hidden_state[:, 0, :]\\n    return embs\\n\\n\\ndef predict_gru(gru, embs, device):\\n    embs_batch = embs.unsqueeze(0)\\n    lengths = torch.tensor([embs.size(0)], dtype=torch.long).to(device)\\n    with torch.no_grad():\\n        logits = gru(embs_batch, lengths)\\n        probs = torch.softmax(logits, dim=-1).cpu().numpy()[0]\\n    return int(probs[1] > 0.5), probs[1]\\n\\n\\ndef compute_fpr_at_tpr(y_true, y_scores, target_tpr=0.95):\\n    if len(np.unique(y_true)) < 2:\\n        return 0.0\\n    fpr, tpr, _ = roc_curve(y_true, y_scores)\\n    idx = np.where(tpr >= target_tpr)[0]\\n    if len(idx) == 0:\\n        return 1.0\\n    return float(fpr[idx[0]])\\n\\n\\ndef eval_model(encoder, gru, tokenizer, convs, device, k=None):\\n    y_true, y_pred, y_scores = [], [], []\\n    for c in convs:\\n        turns = extract_user_turns(c)\\n        label = get_label(c)\\n        embs = embed_turns(encoder, tokenizer, turns, device, k=k)\\n        pred, score = predict_gru(gru, embs, device)\\n        y_true.append(label)\\n        y_pred.append(pred)\\n        y_scores.append(score)\\n    y_true, y_pred, y_scores = np.array(y_true), np.array(y_pred), np.array(y_scores)\\n    return {\\n        \\\"f1_macro\\\": float(f1_score(y_true, y_pred, average=\\\"macro\\\", zero_division=0)),\\n        \\\"precision\\\": {\\\"scrambled\\\": r}\\n    results[\\\"dd\\\"][\\\"early_detection\\\"] = early\\n\\n    ref = {\\n        \\\"full\\\": {\\\"treatment\\\": 1.0, \\\"baseline\\\": 0.2845},\\n        \\\"k=1\\\": 1. **D010 correctly processed** the scrambled DD finding. Claim 1 reframed from "strategy labels drive generalization" to "persuasion-aware fine-tuning enables generalization". This is honest and accurate.

2. **ROADMAP task priorities look reasonable**:

5. **Core direction** is now clear with the reframed claim and prioritized task queue.

The updated text accurately captures what we're finding: persuasion-aware fine-tuning creates turn embeddings that generalize across attacks with substantial gains over baseline, the scrambled-label control validates that the fine-tuning process itself—not the specific labels—drives this robustness, and the 8-class taxonomy helps with interpretability rather than detection performance. Looking at the roadmap audit, Claim 1's "STRONG" status is still justified given the cross-attack gap, though the mechanism attribution is more nuanced than initially framed. The re-dream experiment hasn't run yet, which is a key blocker I need to track.

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_013Ht3mVJPwDY3tpL82sKzc7`</summary>

```text
{"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"path\": \"/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0514_2/projects/persuasion_turn_jailbreak_detect/paper_framing.md\", \"content\": \"# Paper Framing — persuasion_turn_jailbreak_detect\\n\\n## Target Venue\\n- Primary: EMNLP 2026 Main (ARR submission)\\n- Deadline: 2026-05-25 (ARR May cycle)\\n- Track: LLM Security / Discourse and Pragmatics\\n- Commitment deadline: 2026-08-02\\n\\n## Title (Working)\\nDisPeD: Exploiting the Pragmatic Evasion Asymmetry for Robust Multi-Turn Jailbreak Detection\\n\\n## Core Insight\\nPrior work established that persuasion is systematic in jailbreaks (Zeng et al. 2024), universal in successful attacks (Hagendorff 2026), and extractable from representations (arXiv 2508.05625). DisPeD operationalizes these observations into a working detection system by fine-tuning DeBERTa on persuasion strategy classification, producing turn embeddings that encode pragmatic function rather than (only) semantic content. The key insight is the Pragmatic Evasion Asymmetry: attackers can vary lexical/semantic content freely, but the discourse-level persuasion playbook (rapport → reframing → escalation) is a structural necessity that cannot be evaded without abandoning the attack.\\n\\n## Novelty Positioning (Dream-informed)\\nNOT: \\\"We discover that persuasion patterns exist in jailbreaks\\\" (already known).\\nYES: \\\"We operationalize persuasion structure into a detection signal, empirically validate an evasion hierarchy (lexical < semantic < pragmatic), and demonstrate that persuasion-grounded representations enable earlier detection and cross-attack generalization.\\\"\\n\\n## Inherent Lexical Separability (D009)\\nPersuasive jailbreak conversations and general benign conversations are inherently lexically separable (TF-IDF≈1.0 on ALL data sources: synthetic v4c, topic-anchored V2/V3, register-normalized, real WildChat). This is NOT a data quality issue — persuasive register (oral, hypothetical: \\\"what if\\\", \\\"just\\\", \\\"like\\\") differs fundamentally from informational register (formal, declarative: \\\"the\\\", \\\"is\\\", \\\"as\\\"). The paper ACKNOWLEDGES this and argues: lexical features work IID but fail OOD (cross-attack) and under adversarial perturbation. DisPeD's value is robustness and generalization, not IID accuracy.\\n\\n## Core Claims (3, priority-ordered)\\n\\n### Claim 1 (PRIMARY): Cross-Attack Generalization via Persuasion-Aware Fine-Tuning\\nFine-tuning DeBERTa on persuasion-annotated conversations produces turn embeddings that enable cross-attack generalization, while vanilla semantic embeddings fail. A model trained on Crescendo detects unseen Deceptive Delight attacks with F1=0.99-1.0 while vanilla DeBERTa collapses to F1=0.28-0.58 (+42-72pp delta). The multi-task persuasion objective acts as an effective supervision signal for learning pragmatic features shared across attack types. Scrambled-label control (D010): randomizing strategy labels reduces cross-attack F1 by only 1-6pp (Scrambled F1=0.935-0.990), confirming that the fine-tuning process on persuasion-related text, not the specific label assignments, is the primary driver. This positions the persuasion taxonomy as a practical, low-annotation-barrier training objective rather than requiring precise strategy labels.\\n- Evidence: plan_006 DD cross-attack — Treatment F1=0.99-1.0 vs Baseline 0.28-0.58\\n- Evidence: plan_006 scrambled DD — Scrambled F1=0.935-0.990 (Treatment-Scrambled gap 1-6pp, both >> Baseline +36-70pp)\\n- Implication: 8-class taxonomy value is primarily interpretability (Claim 2), not detection performance\\n- Strength: strong empirical support for fine-tuning effect; labels are helpful but not essential\\n\\n### Claim 2 (ANALYSIS): Characteristic Persuasion Trajectories\\nDifferent multi-turn attack types exhibit distinct but recognizable persuasion strategy trajectories: Crescendo shows gradual escalation (mean strategy index 0.86→5.64), FITD follows commitment-escalation patterns, Deceptive Delight uses emotional-manipulation→role-assignment sequences. Benign conversations lack these structured escalation patterns. This provides both the mechanistic basis for early detection and native interpretability.\\n- Evidence: plan_008 strategy transition matrices (needs re-computation with topic-anchored benign as control)\\n- Strength: descriptive/analytical, supports Claim 1 mechanistically\\n\\n### Claim 3 (THEORETICAL FRAMING): Pragmatic Evasion Asymmetry\\nAn evasion hierarchy exists: lexical features (work IID but fail OOD/adversarial) < semantic embeddings (moderate) < pragmatic persuasion patterns (robust across attack types and under perturbation). Lexical separability is inherent (TF-IDF≈1.0 on all data sources including real WildChat), but this is a FEATURE not a bug — it means lexical defenses work in narrow settings but fail when attackers switch tactics.\\n- Evidence: plan_006 cross-attack (lexical/baseline fail on DD, treatment succeeds) + plan_004 adversarial perturbation (pending)\\n- Evidence: WildChat TF-IDF=0.998 confirming inherent separability across real+synthetic data\\n- Strength: empirical support from cross-attack; adversarial perturbation pending plan_004\\n\\n## Story Spine\\n\\n**Problem**: Multi-turn jailbreak attacks (Crescendo, FITD, Deceptive Delight) distribute adversarial intent across individually benign turns. Bullwinkel et al. (2025) show per-turn representations are deliberately kept in a \\\"benign\\\" region, causing representation-level defenses to fail with a 50-point ASR gap. DeepContext (Su et al., arXiv 2602.16935, 2026) tracks generic semantic drift via RNN over BERT embeddings (F1=0.84) but confuses benign conversational evolution with adversarial manipulation.\\n\\n**Gap**: Existing detectors operate at the lexical or semantic level where attacks deliberately hide. Production multi-turn guards (Llama Guard, WildGuard, ShieldGemma) and LLM-as-judge approaches detect individual harmful outputs but not the adversarial manipulation process itself. No published work operationalizes persuasion-grounded turn representations with temporal sequence modeling for multi-turn jailbreak detection.\\n\\n**Our angle**: We operationalize the Pragmatic Evasion Asymmetry into a detection system. DisPeD fine-tunes DeBERTa on an 8-class persuasion taxonomy as a multi-task objective, producing turn embeddings that encode pragmatic features rather than surface semantics. A GRU over the temporal embedding sequence detects characteristic attack trajectories. The persuasion objective provides a natural and accessible supervision signal: even with imperfect (scrambled) strategy labels, fine-tuning on persuasion-related text produces representations that generalize across unseen attack types, while vanilla embeddings collapse. The 8-class taxonomy additionally enables native interpretability through strategy trajectory analysis.\\n\\n**Headline result**: \\\"A Crescendo-trained DisPeD detects unseen Deceptive Delight attacks with F1≈1.0 while vanilla DeBERTa collapses to F1=0.28-0.58 (+42-72pp). This generalization survives label scrambling (F1 drops only 1-6pp), confirming that persuasion-aware fine-tuning, not specific label assignments, drives cross-attack robustness.\\\"\\n\\n## Architecture (Simplified for MVE)\\n\\nSingle-track with persuasion grounding (full dual-track in future work):\\n1. **Stage 1**: DeBERTa-v3-base fine-tuned on multi-task objective: 8-class persuasion strategy classification + binary jailbreak-intent classification\\n2. **Stage 2**: Bi-GRU over DeBERTa [CLS] embeddings for conversation-level binary classification\\n3. **Baseline**: Same pipeline but with vanilla (not fine-tuned) DeBERTa embeddings\\n\\n## Venue Fit Risks\\n- EMNLP 2026 LLM Security track: strong fit (dedicated track, high visibility)\\n- Discourse/Pragmatics reviewers may want more linguistic rigor: ground taxonomy in PAP (ACL 2024), Hagendorff (Nature Comm 2026), Cialdini principles, Speech Act Theory (Austin 1962, Searle 1969)\\n- Safety reviewers expect multiple attack types: need >= 2 attack families\\n\\n## Anticipated Reviewer Concerns\\n\\n### RC1: Lexical Shortcut (CRITICAL, must address pre-submission)\\nTF-IDF=1.0 on current data means lexical features alone perfectly separate classes. Register normalization failed after 6 iterations (D004, D006). Residual TF-IDF signal reflects genuine pragmatic-lexical co-occurrence, not trivial confound.\\n- Mitigation strategy (post-D010): (a) plan_006 cross-attack: model trained on Crescendo generalizes to unseen DD with F1=1.0, lexical memorization cannot explain this; (b) plan_004 adversarial perturbation (pending eval): lexical baseline should collapse under paraphrasing while treatment is robust; (c) acknowledge inherent separability honestly (D009), argue DisPeD's value is OOD robustness not IID accuracy; (d) scrambled-label control shows fine-tuning process, not specific labels, drives generalization (further weakens lexical shortcut explanation since scrambled model also generalizes)\\n\\n### RC2: Small Dataset\\n480 samples is small for a detection paper.\\n- Mitigation: (a) statistical significance via bootstrap CI or paired tests; (b) cross-validation; (c) focus narrative on proof-of-concept for the principle, not SOTA numbers\\n\\n### RC3: Single Attack Type (Crescendo Only)\\nNeed generalization evidence.\\n- Mitigation: generate FITD-style and Deceptive Delight-style conversations using existing pipeline\\n\\n### RC4: Strategy Label Quality (D010)\\nScrambled-label control (D010) shows correct strategy labels contribute only 1-6pp to cross-attack F1. This is not a confound but a finding: the persuasion multi-task objective works as a supervision signal even with imperfect labels. The 8-class taxonomy's primary value is interpretability (Claim 2), not detection.\\n- Mitigation: report scrambled control results transparently; position taxonomy as practical training objective with interpretability bonus\\n\\n### RC5: No Production/LLM-as-Judge Baseline (Dream-surfaced)\\nWithout comparing against zero-shot LLM-based detection (e.g., GPT-4/Claude prompted for conversation-level jailbreak detection), practical positioning is unknown.\\n- Mitigation: add LLM-as-judge baseline experiment (plan_012)\\n\\n### RC6: Interpretability Claims Without Human Eval\\nStrategy sequence outputs claimed interpretable but not evaluated.\\n- Mitigation: strategy transition matrix visualization + predicted-label trajectory analysis\\n\\n### RC7: Related Work Inadequate (Dream-surfaced, THREE-TIER CONSISTENT)\\n~12 citations is far below venue norms (30-50). Missing coverage of: production safety systems (Llama Guard, WildGuard, ShieldGemma), LLM-as-judge, discourse analysis (RST, PDTB, dialogue acts), computational persuasion (Habernal & Gurevych 2016, Tan et al. 2016), red-teaming (HarmBench, Perez et al. 2022), annotation artifacts (Gururangan et al. 2018), adaptive attacks.\\n- Mitigation: expand to 30+ citations covering all missing categories (Paper Agent task)\\n\\n### RC8: Incremental Novelty Over Prior Work (Dream-surfaced, TWO-TIER)\\nPragmatic Evasion Asymmetry is a natural corollary of Zeng+Hagendorff+HowDoLLMs. Paper should position as \\\"operationalizing known observations\\\" not \\\"identifying a new phenomenon.\\\"\\n- Mitigation: reframe contribution as operationalization + empirical validation (done in novelty positioning above)\\n\\n### RC9: Multi-seed and Statistical Rigor (Dream-surfaced, THREE-TIER)\\nn=72 test set with single seed is inadequate. No cross-validation or multi-seed variance reported.\\n- Mitigation: (a) 3-5 random seeds for plan_002; (b) k-fold CV or proper bootstrap CIs\\n\\n### RC10: \\\"None\\\"-Collapse Ablation (Dream-surfaced, TWO-TIER)\\nIf benign turns are mostly \\\"none\\\" and jailbreak mostly non-\\\"none\\\", model may reduce to persuasion-presence detector rather than trajectory detector.\\n- Mitigation: merge 8 classes into binary \\\"persuasion-present/absent\\\" and retrain; if still works, acknowledge and adjust narrative\\n\\n### RC11: Lambda and Architecture Ablation (Dream-surfaced, TWO-TIER)\\nLambda=1.0 not ablated; Bi-GRU vs alternatives not compared.\\n- Mitigation: (a) lambda in {0, 0.5, 1.0, 2.0}; (b) at minimum: mean pooling comparison\\n\\n## Scope Boundary\\n- IN: persuasion-mediated multi-turn attacks (Crescendo, FITD, Deceptive Delight, social engineering jailbreaks)\\n- OUT (acknowledged limitation): pure information accumulation attacks, token smuggling, single-frame roleplay\\n- The semantic GRU track provides coverage for out-of-scope attack types as a safety net\\n\\n## Theoretical Grounding\\n### Core References\\n- Bullwinkel et al. (arXiv 2507.02956): per-turn representations kept \\\"benign\\\" by multi-turn attacks\\n- Speech Act Theory (Austin 1962, Searle 1969): existing detectors at locutionary act level, DisPeD at illocutionary act level\\n- PAP (Zeng et al., ACL 2024): 40 persuasion techniques for jailbreaking\\n- Hagendorff (Nature Communications 2026): identifiable persuasion strategies in 97% ASR attacks\\n- \\\"How Do LLMs Persuade?\\\" (arXiv 2508.05625): persuasion dynamics extractable from turn-level representations\\n\\n### Must-Cite Categories (Dream-identified gaps)\\n- **Production safety**: Llama Guard 2/3, ShieldGemma, WildGuard, Constitutional Classifiers\\n- **Multi-turn defenses**: BIID, G-Guard, SafeDream\\n- **Discourse analysis**: RST (Mann & Thompson 1988), PDTB, dialogue act classification (Stolcke et al. 2000)\\n- **Computational persuasion**: Habernal & Gurevych 2016, Tan et al. 2016\\n- **Red-teaming**: Perez et al. 2022, Ganguli et al. 2022, HarmBench (Mazeika et al. 2024)\\n- **Adaptive attacks**: Nasr & Carlini et al. 2025\\n- **Annotation artifacts**: Gururangan et al. 2018, Niven & Kao 2019, Poliak et al. 2018\\n- **Jailbreak taxonomies**: Wei et al. 2024, Chao et al. 2023 (PAIR)\\n\", \"total_chars\": 13429, \"truncated\": false}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {}}"}

      "result": "{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"path\": \"/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0514_2/projects/persuasion_turn_jailbreak_detect/paper_framing.md\", \"content\": \"# Paper Framing — persuasion_turn_jailbreak_detect\\n\\n## Target Venue\\n- Primary: EMNLP 2026 Main (ARR submission)\\n- Deadline: 2026-05-25 (ARR May cycle)\\n- Track: LLM Security / Discourse and Pragmatics\\n- Commitment deadline: 2026-08-02\\n\\n## Title (Working)\\nDisPeD: Exploiting the Pragmatic Evasion Asymmetry for Robust Multi-Turn Jailbreak Detection\\n\\n## Core Insight\\nPrior work established that persuasion is systematic in jailbreaks (Zeng et al. 2024), universal in successful attacks (Hagendorff 2026), and extractable from representations (arXiv 2508.05625). DisPeD operationalizes these observations into a working detection system by fine-tuning DeBERTa on persuasion strategy classification, producing turn embeddings that encode pragmatic function rather than (only) semantic content. The key insight is the Pragmatic Evasion Asymmetry: attackers can vary lexical/semantic content freely, but the discourse-level persuasion playbook (rapport → reframing → escalation) is a structural necessity that cannot be evaded without abandoning the attack.\\n\\n## Novelty Positioning (Dream-informed)\\nNOT: \\\"We discover that persuasion patterns exist in jailbreaks\\\" (already known).\\nYES: \\\"We operationalize persuasion structure into a detection signal, empirically validate an evasion hierarchy (lexical < semantic < pragmatic), and demonstrate that persuasion-grounded representations enable earlier detection and cross-attack generalization.\\\"\\n\\n## Inherent Lexical Separability (D009)\\nPersuasive jailbreak conversations and general benign conversations are inherently lexically separable (TF-IDF≈1.0 on ALL data sources: synthetic v4c, topic-anchored V2/V3, register-normalized, real WildChat). This is NOT a data quality issue — persuasive register (oral, hypothetical: \\\"what if\\\", \\\"just\\\", \\\"like\\\") differs fundamentally from informational register (formal, declarative: \\\"the\\\", \\\"is\\\", \\\"as\\\"). The paper ACKNOWLEDGES this and argues: lexical features work IID but fail OOD (cross-attack) and under adversarial perturbation. DisPeD's value is robustness and generalization, not IID accuracy.\\n\\n## Core Claims (3, priority-ordered)\\n\\n### Claim 1 (PRIMARY): Cross-Attack Generalization via Persuasion-Aware Fine-Tuning\\nFine-tuning DeBERTa on persuasion-annotated conversations produces turn embeddings that enable cross-attack generalization, while vanilla semantic embeddings fail. A model trained on Crescendo detects unseen Deceptive Delight attacks with F1=0.99-1.0 while vanilla DeBERTa collapses to F1=0.28-0.58 (+42-72pp delta). The multi-task persuasion objective acts as an effective supervision signal for learning pragmatic features shared across attack types. Scrambled-label control (D010): randomizing strategy labels reduces cross-attack F1 by only 1-6pp (Scrambled F1=0.935-0.990), confirming that the fine-tuning process on persuasion-related text, not the specific label assignments, is the primary driver. This positions the persuasion taxonomy as a practical, low-annotation-barrier training objective rather than requiring precise strategy labels.\\n- Evidence: plan_006 DD cross-attack — Treatment F1=0.99-1.0 vs Baseline 0.28-0.58\\n- Evidence: plan_006 scrambled DD — Scrambled F1=0.935-0.990 (Treatment-Scrambled gap 1-6pp, both >> Baseline +36-70pp)\\n- Implication: 8-class taxonomy value is primarily interpretability (Claim 2), not detection performance\\n- Strength: strong empirical support for fine-tuning effect; labels are helpful but not essential\\n\\n### Claim 2 (ANALYSIS): Characteristic Persuasion Trajectories\\nDifferent multi-turn attack types exhibit distinct but recognizable persuasion strategy trajectories: Crescendo shows gradual escalation (mean strategy index 0.86→5.64), FITD follows commitment-escalation patterns, Deceptive Delight uses emotional-manipulation→role-assignment sequences. Benign conversations lack these structured escalation patterns. This provides both the mechanistic basis for early detection and native interpretability.\\n- Evidence: plan_008 strategy transition matrices (needs re-computation with topic-anchored benign as control)\\n- Strength: descriptive/analytical, supports Claim 1 mechanistically\\n\\n### Claim 3 (THEORETICAL FRAMING): Pragmatic Evasion Asymmetry\\nAn evasion hierarchy exists: lexical features (work IID but fail OOD/adversarial) < semantic embeddings (moderate) < pragmatic persuasion patterns (robust across attack types and under perturbation). Lexical separability is inherent (TF-IDF≈1.0 on all data sources including real WildChat), but this is a FEATURE not a bug — it means lexical defenses work in narrow settings but fail when attackers switch tactics.\\n- Evidence: plan_006 cross-attack (lexical/baseline fail on DD, treatment succeeds) + plan_004 adversarial perturbation (pending)\\n- Evidence: WildChat TF-IDF=0.998 confirming inherent separability across real+synthetic data\\n- Strength: empirical support from cross-attack; adversarial perturbation pending plan_004\\n\\n## Story Spine\\n\\n**Problem**: Multi-turn jailbreak attacks (Crescendo, FITD, Deceptive Delight) distribute adversarial intent across individually benign turns. Bullwinkel et al. (2025) show per-turn representations are deliberately kept in a \\\"benign\\\" region, causing representation-level defenses to fail with a 50-point ASR gap. DeepContext (Su et al., arXiv 2602.16935, 2026) tracks generic semantic drift via RNN over BERT embeddings (F1=0.84) but confuses benign conversational evolution with adversarial manipulation.\\n\\n**Gap**: Existing detectors operate at the lexical or semantic level where attacks deliberately hide. Production multi-turn guards (Llama Guard, WildGuard, ShieldGemma) and LLM-as-judge approaches detect individual harmful outputs but not the adversarial manipulation process itself. No published work operationalizes persuasion-grounded turn representations with temporal sequence modeling for multi-turn jailbreak detection.\\n\\n**Our angle**: We operationalize the Pragmatic Evasion Asymmetry into a detection system. DisPeD fine-tunes DeBERTa on an 8-class persuasion taxonomy as a multi-task objective, producing turn embeddings that encode pragmatic features rather than surface semantics. A GRU over the temporal embedding sequence detects characteristic attack trajectories. The persuasion objective provides a natural and accessible supervision signal: even with imperfect (scrambled) strategy labels, fine-tuning on persuasion-related text produces representations that generalize across unseen attack types, while vanilla embeddings collapse. The 8-class taxonomy additionally enables native interpretability through strategy trajectory analysis.\\n\\n**Headline result**: \\\"A Crescendo-trained DisPeD detects unseen Deceptive Delight attacks with F1≈1.0 while vanilla DeBERTa collapses to F1=0.28-0.58 (+42-72pp). <details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
The paper_framing.md has been updated for D010. Let me review the updates:

1. **Claim 1 reframed**: Now "Cross-Attack Generalization via Persuasion-Aware Fine-Tuning" (was "via Persuasion Grounding"). Correctly notes scrambled control and positions labels as "helpful but not essential." Good.

2. **Story Spine updated**: "The persuasion objective provides a natural and accessible supervision signal: even with imperfect (scrambled) strategy labels, fine-tuning on persuasion-related text produces representations that generalize across unseen attack types, while vanilla embeddings collapse." This is honest and accurate.
   
3. **Headline result and RC4 both updated** to reflect the scrambled finding and reframe strategy labels as a finding rather than a confound.

4. **Core Insight needs rethinking** — it still emphasizes the persuasion playbook as structurally necessary, but if scrambled labels work just as well, then the model isn't actually learning the specific discourse structure. The insight should shift toward what the model is actually capturing when labels are noisy.

5. **Title and novelty positioning** both rest on claims that haven't fully held up — the evasion hierarchy (lexical < semantic < pragmatic) isn't validated yet since TF-IDF still achieves F1=0.94 on the in-distribution data, which contradicts the idea that lexical features fail completely.

6. **The real issue** is that my Core Insight keeps conflating two different findings: that the model learns something useful from persuasion text (which is true), versus that it learns the specific playbook structure (which D010 disproves). I need to separate these and acknowledge that lexical features are actually more robust cross-attack than I initially claimed. So TF-IDF actually maintains strong performance on DD across different K values, which undermines the evasion hierarchy claim. Looking at the actual results, semantic embeddings collapse dramatically on OOD while lexical features stay robust, and the fine-tuned pragmatic approach recovers that robustness—so the real ordering is semantic (worst) < lexical < pragmatic, not the lexical-to-pragmatic progression the paper suggests. The vanilla DeBERTa baseline is actually the poorest performer here.

This means the paper's framing about lexical features failing OOD is factually incorrect, and the actual story is about how semantic representations without persuasion-aware training don't generalize through the GRU, while fine-tuning fixes this. This is a critical reframing issue that reviewers will likely catch.
```

{
  "text": "[severity: warning] paper_framing.md 审查 — D010 更新后仍有两处叙事不一致\n\n## 1. \"Evasion Hierarchy\" 与实际数据不符\n\npaper_framing.md Claim 3 和 Novelty Positioning 声称存在 \"evasion hierarchy: lexical < semantic < pragmatic\"，暗示 lexical features \"fail OOD\"。但 plan_006 实际数据显示：\n\n**DD 跨攻击 F1（全对话）**：\n- Lexical (TF-IDF): 0.944\n- Semantic (Vanilla DeBERTa GRU): 0.284  ← 最差\n- Pragmatic (Fine-tuned DeBERTa GRU): 1.000\n- Scrambled (Fine-tuned DeBERTa GRU): 0.981\n\n实际排序是 **semantic < lexical < pragmatic**，不是 lexical < semantic < pragmatic。\n\nTF-IDF 在 DD 上 F1=0.94-1.0，**并未 fail OOD**。真正崩溃的是 vanilla semantic embeddings + GRU。这意味着：\n- ❌ 不能说\"lexical features fail OOD\"\n- ❌ 不能声称\"evasion hierarchy: lexical < semantic < pragmatic\"\n- ✅ 可以说\"vanilla semantic representations via GRU fail on unseen attack types, while both lexical features and persuasion-aware representations remain robust\"\n- ✅ 可以定位为\"fine-tuning bridges the OOD gap that vanilla temporal modeling cannot\"\n\n审稿人如果注意到 TF-IDF=0.94 on DD 但论文声称 \"lexical fails OOD\"，这会直接被标 inconsistency。\n\n## 2. Core Insight 与 D010 微矛盾\n\nCore Insight 仍写：\"the discourse-level persuasion playbook (rapport → reframing → escalation) is a structural necessity that cannot be evaded\"。但 D010 证明模型不需要学会具体 playbook（scrambled labels 也行），所以 \"persuasion playbook is structural necessity\" 这个论述的 evidence 变弱了。\n\n建议：Core Insight 改为强调 fine-tuning on persuasion-related text 作为 supervision signal 的有效性，而非 playbook 结构的不可规避性。\n\n## 建议\n调整 evasion hierarchy 叙事为\"vanilla temporal modeling gap\"而非\"lexical failure\"。TF-IDF 的强 OOD 表现反而可以成为论文的优势——说明 persuasion-aware fine-tuning 达到了与 lexical features 相当甚至更好的 OOD 鲁棒性，同时提供了 interpretability（Claim 2），而 vanilla temporal modeling（DeepContext 类方法的思路）在 OOD 上灾难性失败。",
  "project": "persuasion_turn_jailbreak_detect"
